Oh, no... no, I wouldn't say I feel guilty. I mean, it was her time, and there wasn't anything anyone could have done, really. I was always a good friend to her, and we had such lovely times together. I try to focus on those good memories instead of dwelling on any... well, any "what ifs."

I don't want to just sit around feeling sorry for myself. \n\nMaybe I just get a little tired a bit faster right now, but the enjoyment is definitely still there. I think it's so important to keep busy and count your blessings, don't you agree, Doctor? I just... I don't want you to think I've given up on everything, because I certainly haven't. Is that the sort of thing you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything related to your friend's passing?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... no, I wouldn't say I feel guilty. I mean, it was her time, and there wasn't anything anyone could have done, really. I was always a good friend to her, and we had such lovely times together. I try to focus on those good memories instead of dwelling on any... well, any \"what ifs.\" \n\nI think it's just natural to feel sad, but guilt? No, I don't think I have any reason to feel that way. I've always tried to do the right thing by the people I care about. I hope that makes sense? I certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm harboring any bad feelings or anything like that. That's a very normal way to look at it, isn't it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find enjoyable, but now feel like a chore or don't bring you as much happiness as they used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I think that might be a bit like what you were asking just a moment ago, isn't it? No, I really wouldn't call anything a chore. I mean, keeping up with the house and my little hobbies... it's just what you do, and I still find joy in them, I promise. \n\nI suppose sometimes it takes just a tiny bit more effort to get started these days, but once I'm doing it, I'm perfectly happy. I've always prided myself on being a hard worker and not making a fuss about things. I hope I'm giving you the right kinds of answers? I'd hate to be difficult or feel like I'm repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, my sleep? No, no trouble at all, thankfully. I've always been a very good sleeper, and I... well, I make sure to keep to my usual bedtime. I think getting a proper night's rest is just so important to keep your strength up, especially right now. I wouldn't want to let myself get all run down and become a worry to anyone. \n\nSo, no, I just close my eyes and sleep right through till morning. That's a good sign, isn't it, Doctor? I'm trying very hard to keep everything as normal as possible, I really am.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, my appetite? Well, I think it's been quite normal, really. I always make sure to sit down and have my meals, just like I always have. It's so important to keep your strength up, isn't it? I wouldn't want to skip meals and get weak or anything like that. \n\nI mean, perhaps the portions are just a tiny bit smaller sometimes, but that's just because I'm not doing quite as much running around these days, I'm sure. I always make a point to finish what's on my plate. I hope that's the right thing to do? I'm really trying my best to take good care of myself, just like you'd want me to, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social situations or gatherings since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I'm avoiding anyone. I still go to my usual get-togethers and... well, I always make sure to chat with the neighbors when I see them. I wouldn't want anyone to think I was being rude or, you know, shutting them out. That wouldn't be very nice of me, would it? \n\nI mean, maybe I don't stay quite as long at coffee hour as I used to, but I always make sure to make an appearance and say my hellos. I hope that's the right way to handle it, Doctor.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Oh, preoccupied? Well, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, naturally, she crosses my mind now and then—we were dear friends, after all. But I certainly don't sit around dwelling on it all day long. That wouldn't be very healthy, would it? 

I always try to keep myself busy with my little chores around the house. I think it's much better to just remember the fond memories and then keep moving forward, don't you agree? I wouldn't want anyone to worry that I'm obsessing or getting stuck in the past. I'm really trying to handle it the right way, Doctor. I hope that's how I should be looking at it?

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Oh, hello Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I really hope I'm not wasting your time—I'm sure you have patients with much bigger problems to deal with. \n\nIt's just, well... I guess I haven't really been myself lately. My friend from choir passed away recently, very suddenly, and... I don't know, it's a bit embarrassing to admit. I know I should be handling it better, but I've been getting all worked up over silly little things. Like, I'll just start crying over the potluck lists. And phone calls I used to handle so easy just feel like too much all of a sudden. I've even started missing some of the church planning things, which I never, ever would have missed before. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that sounds a bit foolish. I hope I'm explaining things okay for you?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been about a month now... yes, right around a month since she passed. I really thought I'd be over the shock of it by now, to be honest. I mean, people lose friends at my age, it's just a part of life, isn't it? I shouldn't be making such a fuss after a whole month. I hope I'm not making too much of it... does that timeline make sense to you, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you normally like to do, such as choir, as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I think I still enjoy it. I really do try to. The singing is always so lovely, and the ladies there are just wonderful. I wouldn't say I've stopped liking it at all. \n\nI mean, maybe I'm just a little quieter during practice lately, but I still love the music. I try my very best to keep a smile on my face for everyone so I don't bring the mood down. I hope that's the right way to handle it? I really don't want to sound like a complainer, especially when everyone is being so nice to me. Does that answer your question alright?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable or fun, but no longer do?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I don't think so. I mean, I still try to keep up with all my usual things around the house. I still like tending to my plants and... and baking when I have the time. I wouldn't say I've stopped finding them fun at all. I really do try to focus on the good things, you know? I don't want to just sit around feeling sorry for myself. \n\nMaybe I just get a little tired a bit faster right now, but the enjoyment is definitely still there. I think it's so important to keep busy and count your blessings, don't you agree, Doctor? I just... I don't want you to think I've given up on everything, because I certainly haven't. Is that the sort of thing you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything related to your friend's passing?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... no, I wouldn't say I feel guilty. That's a very normal way to look at it, isn't it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find enjoyable, but now feel like a chore or don't bring you as much happiness as they used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I've always prided myself on being a hard worker and not making a fuss about things. I hope I'm giving you the right kinds of answers? Unexpected times? Well... no, not really. I mean, maybe a little thought will pop in if I see something that reminds me of her, like a certain type of flower at the grocery store, but I just smile and keep walking. I wouldn't say it catches me off guard or anything like that.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Oh, hello Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I really hope I'm not wasting your time—I'm sure you have patients with much bigger problems to deal with. \n\nIt's just, well... I guess I haven't really been myself lately. My friend from choir passed away recently, very suddenly, and... I don't know, it's a bit embarrassing to admit. I know I should be handling it better, but I've been getting all worked up over silly little things. Like, I'll just start crying over the potluck lists. And phone calls I used to handle so easy just feel like too much all of a sudden. I've even started missing some of the church planning things, which I never, ever would have missed before. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that sounds a bit foolish. I hope I'm explaining things okay for you?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been about a month now... yes, right around a month since she passed. I really thought I'd be over the shock of it by now, to be honest. I mean, people lose friends at my age, it's just a part of life, isn't it? I shouldn't be making such a fuss after a whole month. I hope I'm not making too much of it... does that timeline make sense to you, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you normally like to do, such as choir, as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I think I still enjoy it. I really do try to. The singing is always so lovely, and the ladies there are just wonderful. I wouldn't say I've stopped liking it at all. \n\nI mean, maybe I'm just a little quieter during practice lately, but I still love the music. I try my very best to keep a smile on my face for everyone so I don't bring the mood down. I hope that's the right way to handle it? I really don't want to sound like a complainer, especially when everyone is being so nice to me. Does that answer your question alright?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable or fun, but no longer do?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I don't think so. I mean, I still try to keep up with all my usual things around the house. I still like tending to my plants and... and baking when I have the time. I wouldn't say I've stopped finding them fun at all. I really do try to focus on the good things, you know? I don't want to just sit around feeling sorry for myself. \n\nMaybe I just get a little tired a bit faster right now, but the enjoyment is definitely still there. I think it's so important to keep busy and count your blessings, don't you agree, Doctor? I just... I don't want you to think I've given up on everything, because I certainly haven't. Is that the sort of thing you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything related to your friend's passing?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... no, I wouldn't say I feel guilty. I mean, it was her time, and there wasn't anything anyone could have done, really. I was always a good friend to her, and we had such lovely times together. I try to focus on those good memories instead of dwelling on any... well, any \"what ifs.\" \n\nI think it's just natural to feel sad, but guilt? No, I don't think I have any reason to feel that way. I've always tried to do the right thing by the people I care about. I hope that makes sense? I certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm harboring any bad feelings or anything like that. That's a very normal way to look at it, isn't it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find enjoyable, but now feel like a chore or don't bring you as much happiness as they used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I think that might be a bit like what you were asking just a moment ago, isn't it? No, I really wouldn't call anything a chore. I mean, keeping up with the house and my little hobbies... it's just what you do, and I still find joy in them, I promise. \n\nI suppose sometimes it takes just a tiny bit more effort to get started these days, but once I'm doing it, I'm perfectly happy. I've always prided myself on being a hard worker and not making a fuss about things. I hope I'm giving you the right kinds of answers? I'd hate to be difficult or feel like I'm repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, my sleep? No, no trouble at all, thankfully. I've always been a very good sleeper, and I... well, I make sure to keep to my usual bedtime. I wouldn't want to skip meals and get weak or anything like that. \n\nI mean, perhaps the portions are just a tiny bit smaller sometimes, but that's just because I'm not doing quite as much running around these days, I'm sure. I always make a point to finish what's on my plate. I hope that's the right thing to do? Oh, goodness, no. No, I really don't do that. I mean, it was... well, it was just her time, and there was really nothing anyone could have done anyway. I think it would be terribly unhelpful to sit around second-guessing things like that, don't you?

I wouldn't say I've stopped liking it at all. \n\nI mean, maybe I'm just a little quieter during practice lately, but I still love the music. I try my very best to keep a smile on my face for everyone so I don't bring the mood down. I hope that's the right way to handle it? I really don't want to sound like a complainer, especially when everyone is being so nice to me. I mean, I still try to keep up with all my usual things around the house. I still like tending to my plants and... and baking when I have the time. I wouldn't say I've stopped finding them fun at all. I really do try to focus on the good things, you know? I don't want to just sit around feeling sorry for myself. \n\nMaybe I just get a little tired a bit faster right now, but the enjoyment is definitely still there. That's a very normal way to look at it, isn't it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find enjoyable, but now feel like a chore or don't bring you as much happiness as they used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I think that might be a bit like what you were asking just a moment ago, isn't it? No, I really wouldn't call anything a chore. I've always prided myself on being a hard worker and not making a fuss about things. I hope I'm giving you the right kinds of answers? Well, I think it's been quite normal, really. I always make sure to sit down and have my meals, just like I always have. It's so important to keep your strength up, isn't it? I wouldn't want to skip meals and get weak or anything like that. \n\nI mean, perhaps the portions are just a tiny bit smaller sometimes, but that's just because I'm not doing quite as much running around these days, I'm sure. I always make a point to finish what's on my plate. I hope that's the right thing to do? I still go to my usual get-togethers and... well, I always make sure to chat with the neighbors when I see them. I wouldn't want anyone to think I was being rude or, you know, shutting them out. That wouldn't be very nice of me, would it? \n\nI mean, maybe I don't stay quite as long at coffee hour as I used to, but I always make sure to make an appearance and say my hellos. I think it's important to keep up with people and put on a brave face, don't you agree? I'm really trying not to let things make it awkward for anyone else. I hope that's the right way to handle it, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: Oh, sad most of the time? No, no, I wouldn't say most of the time. I mean, of course, I have my moments, just like anyone would, but I really try not to dwell on things. I think it's so important to focus on the positive, don't you? \n\nI always try to keep a smile on my face, especially when I'm around others. I wouldn't want to bring anyone else down or make them feel uncomfortable because of me. That wouldn't be very fair to them. So I just... I keep busy, and I try to look on the bright side. That's the best way to handle things, isn't it, Doctor? I'm really doing my best to be strong and not let it take over.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself preoccupied with thoughts of your friend who passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, preoccupied? Well, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, naturally, she crosses my mind now and then—we were dear friends, after all. But I certainly don't sit around dwelling on it all day long. That wouldn't be very healthy, would it? \n\nI always try to keep myself busy with my little chores around the house. I think it's much better to just remember the fond memories and then keep moving forward, don't you agree? I wouldn't want anyone to worry that I'm obsessing or getting stuck in the past. I'm really trying to handle it the right way, Doctor. I hope that's how I should be looking at it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself thinking about your friend at unexpected times?\n\nPatient: Unexpected times? Well... no, not really. I mean, maybe a little thought will pop in if I see something that reminds me of her, like a certain type of flower at the grocery store, but I just smile and keep walking. I wouldn't say it catches me off guard or anything like that. \n\nI really try to stay focused on whatever task I'm doing, you know? It wouldn't be very productive to just be daydreaming or getting distracted in the middle of the day. I think it's better to keep my mind on the present, don't you? I wouldn't want you to think I'm losing my focus or anything. I'm doing pretty well at keeping things normal, right, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself wishing you could have done something to prevent your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I really don't do that. I mean, it was... well, it was just her time, and there was really nothing anyone could have done anyway. Does that answer your question alright?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable or fun, but no longer do?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I don't think so. I mean, I still try to keep up with all my usual things around the house. I still like tending to my plants and... and baking when I have the time. I wouldn't say I've stopped finding them fun at all. I really do try to focus on the good things, you know? I don't want to just sit around feeling sorry for myself. \n\nMaybe I just get a little tired a bit faster right now, but the enjoyment is definitely still there. I think it's so important to keep busy and count your blessings, don't you agree, Doctor? I just... I don't want you to think I've given up on everything, because I certainly haven't. Is that the sort of thing you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything related to your friend's passing?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... no, I wouldn't say I feel guilty. I mean, it was her time, and there wasn't anything anyone could have done, really. I was always a good friend to her, and we had such lovely times together. I try to focus on those good memories instead of dwelling on any... well, any \"what ifs.\" \n\nI think it's just natural to feel sad, but guilt? No, I don't think I have any reason to feel that way. I've always tried to do the right thing by the people I care about. I hope that makes sense? I certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm harboring any bad feelings or anything like that. That's a very normal way to look at it, isn't it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find enjoyable, but now feel like a chore or don't bring you as much happiness as they used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I think that might be a bit like what you were asking just a moment ago, isn't it? No, I really wouldn't call anything a chore. I mean, keeping up with the house and my little hobbies... it's just what you do, and I still find joy in them, I promise. \n\nI suppose sometimes it takes just a tiny bit more effort to get started these days, but once I'm doing it, I'm perfectly happy. I've always prided myself on being a hard worker and not making a fuss about things. I hope I'm giving you the right kinds of answers? I'd hate to be difficult or feel like I'm repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, my sleep? No, no trouble at all, thankfully. I've always been a very good sleeper, and I... well, I make sure to keep to my usual bedtime. I think getting a proper night's rest is just so important to keep your strength up, especially right now. I wouldn't want to let myself get all run down and become a worry to anyone. \n\nSo, no, I just close my eyes and sleep right through till morning. That's a good sign, isn't it, Doctor? I'm trying very hard to keep everything as normal as possible, I really am.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, my appetite? Well, I think it's been quite normal, really. I always make sure to sit down and have my meals, just like I always have. It's so important to keep your strength up, isn't it? I wouldn't want to skip meals and get weak or anything like that. \n\nI mean, perhaps the portions are just a tiny bit smaller sometimes, but that's just because I'm not doing quite as much running around these days, I'm sure. I always make a point to finish what's on my plate. I hope that's the right thing to do? I'm really trying my best to take good care of myself, just like you'd want me to, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social situations or gatherings since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I'm avoiding anyone. I still go to my usual get-togethers and... well, I always make sure to chat with the neighbors when I see them. I wouldn't want anyone to think I was being rude or, you know, shutting them out. That wouldn't be very nice of me, would it? \n\nI mean, maybe I don't stay quite as long at coffee hour as I used to, but I always make sure to make an appearance and say my hellos. I think it's important to keep up with people and put on a brave face, don't you agree? I'm really trying not to let things make it awkward for anyone else. I hope that's the right way to handle it, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: Oh, sad most of the time? No, no, I wouldn't say most of the time. I mean, of course, I have my moments, just like anyone would, but I really try not to dwell on things. I think it's so important to focus on the positive, don't you? \n\nI always try to keep a smile on my face, especially when I'm around others. I wouldn't want to bring anyone else down or make them feel uncomfortable because of me. That wouldn't be very fair to them. So I just... I keep busy, and I try to look on the bright side. That's the best way to handle things, isn't it, Doctor? I'm really doing my best to be strong and not let it take over.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself preoccupied with thoughts of your friend who passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, preoccupied? Well, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, naturally, she crosses my mind now and then—we were dear friends, after all. But I certainly don't sit around dwelling on it all day long. That wouldn't be very healthy, would it? \n\nI always try to keep myself busy with my little chores around the house. I think it's much better to just remember the fond memories and then keep moving forward, don't you agree? I wouldn't want anyone to worry that I'm obsessing or getting stuck in the past. I'm really trying to handle it the right way, Doctor. I hope that's how I should be looking at it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself thinking about your friend at unexpected times?\n\nPatient: Unexpected times? Well... no, not really. I mean, maybe a little thought will pop in if I see something that reminds me of her, like a certain type of flower at the grocery store, but I just smile and keep walking. I wouldn't say it catches me off guard or anything like that. \n\nI really try to stay focused on whatever task I'm doing, you know? It wouldn't be very productive to just be daydreaming or getting distracted in the middle of the day. I think it's better to keep my mind on the present, don't you? I wouldn't want you to think I'm losing my focus or anything. I'm doing pretty well at keeping things normal, right, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself wishing you could have done something to prevent your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I really don't do that. I mean, it was... well, it was just her time, and there was really nothing anyone could have done anyway. I think it would be terribly unhelpful to sit around second-guessing things like that, don't you? \n\nI always tell myself that we just have to accept these things as a part of life. I certainly wouldn't want to make myself sick with guilt over something I couldn't change. That wouldn't be very sensible of me, would it? I'm just trying to be practical and keep a level head about it all. I hope that's the right way to look at it?\n\nDoctor: How overwhelming do you find your emotional reactions to be when you think about your friend who passed away?\n\nPatient: Overwhelming? Oh, heavens, no. I wouldn't say that at all. I mean... of course, I feel a little sad sometimes, just a quiet sort of sadness, but I certainly don't let it overwhelm me. I never just break down crying or anything dramatic like that. \n\nI always make sure to keep myself composed. It just wouldn't be appropriate to fall to pieces, would it? I try to just take a nice deep breath and find something useful to do around the house instead. I really want to make sure I'm handling all this the proper way. Being strong and keeping my chin up... that's what you'd recommend, isn't it, Doctor?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Intense? Oh, no, not intense. I wouldn't use that word at all. It's just... well, it's just a normal amount of sadness, I suppose. Just a little heavy feeling sometimes, but nothing I can't handle.

I wouldn't want to let myself get all run down and become a worry to anyone. \n\nSo, no, I just close my eyes and sleep right through till morning. That's a good sign, isn't it, Doctor? I'm trying very hard to keep everything as normal as possible, I really am.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, my appetite? Well, I think it's been quite normal, really. I always make sure to sit down and have my meals, just like I always have. It's so important to keep your strength up, isn't it? I always make a point to finish what's on my plate. I hope that's the right thing to do? I'm really trying my best to take good care of myself, just like you'd want me to, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social situations or gatherings since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I'm avoiding anyone. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, heavens no. I wouldn't say a part of me died. That sounds so... so drastic, doesn't it? I mean, I miss her terribly, of course, but I'm still very much myself. 

I still have my garden to tend to, and my church group, and all my little routines. I think it's so important to keep living your life, even when things are difficult. I'd never want to just give up or lose myself just because I'm going through a rough patch. That wouldn't be very sensible, would it? I'm just trying to keep busy and carry on as best I can. That's what you're supposed to do, right?

Oh, no, I don't think so. I mean, maybe just a tiny bit right at the very beginning, but I've been very careful not to isolate myself. I know it's not good for a person to just sit alone in the house all day.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Oh, hello Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I really hope I'm not wasting your time—I'm sure you have patients with much bigger problems to deal with. \n\nIt's just, well... I guess I haven't really been myself lately. My friend from choir passed away recently, very suddenly, and... I don't know, it's a bit embarrassing to admit. I know I should be handling it better, but I've been getting all worked up over silly little things. Like, I'll just start crying over the potluck lists. And phone calls I used to handle so easy just feel like too much all of a sudden. I've even started missing some of the church planning things, which I never, ever would have missed before. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that sounds a bit foolish. I hope I'm explaining things okay for you?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been about a month now... yes, right around a month since she passed. I really thought I'd be over the shock of it by now, to be honest. I mean, people lose friends at my age, it's just a part of life, isn't it? I shouldn't be making such a fuss after a whole month. I hope I'm not making too much of it... does that timeline make sense to you, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you normally like to do, such as choir, as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I think I still enjoy it. I really do try to. The singing is always so lovely, and the ladies there are just wonderful. I wouldn't say I've stopped liking it at all. \n\nI mean, maybe I'm just a little quieter during practice lately, but I still love the music. I try my very best to keep a smile on my face for everyone so I don't bring the mood down. I hope that's the right way to handle it? I really don't want to sound like a complainer, especially when everyone is being so nice to me. Does that answer your question alright?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable or fun, but no longer do?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I don't think so. I mean, I still try to keep up with all my usual things around the house. I still like tending to my plants and... and baking when I have the time. I wouldn't say I've stopped finding them fun at all. I really do try to focus on the good things, you know? I don't want to just sit around feeling sorry for myself. \n\nMaybe I just get a little tired a bit faster right now, but the enjoyment is definitely still there. I think it's so important to keep busy and count your blessings, don't you agree, Doctor? I just... I don't want you to think I've given up on everything, because I certainly haven't. Is that the sort of thing you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything related to your friend's passing?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... no, I wouldn't say I feel guilty. I mean, it was her time, and there wasn't anything anyone could have done, really. I was always a good friend to her, and we had such lovely times together. I try to focus on those good memories instead of dwelling on any... well, any \"what ifs.\" \n\nI think it's just natural to feel sad, but guilt? No, I don't think I have any reason to feel that way. I've always tried to do the right thing by the people I care about. I hope that makes sense? I certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm harboring any bad feelings or anything like that. That's a very normal way to look at it, isn't it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find enjoyable, but now feel like a chore or don't bring you as much happiness as they used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I think that might be a bit like what you were asking just a moment ago, isn't it? No, I really wouldn't call anything a chore. I mean, keeping up with the house and my little hobbies... it's just what you do, and I still find joy in them, I promise. \n\nI suppose sometimes it takes just a tiny bit more effort to get started these days, but once I'm doing it, I'm perfectly happy. I've always prided myself on being a hard worker and not making a fuss about things. I hope I'm giving you the right kinds of answers? I'd hate to be difficult or feel like I'm repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, my sleep? No, no trouble at all, thankfully. I've always been a very good sleeper, and I... well, I make sure to keep to my usual bedtime. I think getting a proper night's rest is just so important to keep your strength up, especially right now. I wouldn't want to let myself get all run down and become a worry to anyone. \n\nSo, no, I just close my eyes and sleep right through till morning. That's a good sign, isn't it, Doctor? I'm trying very hard to keep everything as normal as possible, I really am.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, my appetite? Well, I think it's been quite normal, really. I always make sure to sit down and have my meals, just like I always have. It's so important to keep your strength up, isn't it? I wouldn't want to skip meals and get weak or anything like that. \n\nI mean, perhaps the portions are just a tiny bit smaller sometimes, but that's just because I'm not doing quite as much running around these days, I'm sure. I always make a point to finish what's on my plate. I hope that's the right thing to do? I'm really trying my best to take good care of myself, just like you'd want me to, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social situations or gatherings since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I'm avoiding anyone. I still go to my usual get-togethers and... well, I always make sure to chat with the neighbors when I see them. I wouldn't want anyone to think I was being rude or, you know, shutting them out. That wouldn't be very nice of me, would it? \n\nI mean, maybe I don't stay quite as long at coffee hour as I used to, but I always make sure to make an appearance and say my hellos. I think it's important to keep up with people and put on a brave face, don't you agree? I'm really trying not to let things make it awkward for anyone else. I hope that's the right way to handle it, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: Oh, sad most of the time? No, no, I wouldn't say most of the time. I mean, of course, I have my moments, just like anyone would, but I really try not to dwell on things. I think it's so important to focus on the positive, don't you? \n\nI always try to keep a smile on my face, especially when I'm around others. I wouldn't want to bring anyone else down or make them feel uncomfortable because of me. That wouldn't be very fair to them. So I just... I keep busy, and I try to look on the bright side. That's the best way to handle things, isn't it, Doctor? I'm really doing my best to be strong and not let it take over.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself preoccupied with thoughts of your friend who passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, preoccupied? Well, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, naturally, she crosses my mind now and then—we were dear friends, after all. But I certainly don't sit around dwelling on it all day long. That wouldn't be very healthy, would it? \n\nI always try to keep myself busy with my little chores around the house. I think it's much better to just remember the fond memories and then keep moving forward, don't you agree? I wouldn't want anyone to worry that I'm obsessing or getting stuck in the past. I'm really trying to handle it the right way, Doctor. I hope that's how I should be looking at it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself thinking about your friend at unexpected times?\n\nPatient: Unexpected times? Well... no, not really. I mean, maybe a little thought will pop in if I see something that reminds me of her, like a certain type of flower at the grocery store, but I just smile and keep walking. I wouldn't say it catches me off guard or anything like that. \n\nI really try to stay focused on whatever task I'm doing, you know? It wouldn't be very productive to just be daydreaming or getting distracted in the middle of the day. I think it's better to keep my mind on the present, don't you? I wouldn't want you to think I'm losing my focus or anything. I'm doing pretty well at keeping things normal, right, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself wishing you could have done something to prevent your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I really don't do that. I mean, it was... well, it was just her time, and there was really nothing anyone could have done anyway. I think it would be terribly unhelpful to sit around second-guessing things like that, don't you? \n\nI always tell myself that we just have to accept these things as a part of life. I certainly wouldn't want to make myself sick with guilt over something I couldn't change. That wouldn't be very sensible of me, would it? I'm just trying to be practical and keep a level head about it all. I hope that's the right way to look at it?\n\nDoctor: How overwhelming do you find your emotional reactions to be when you think about your friend who passed away?\n\nPatient: Overwhelming? Oh, heavens, no. I wouldn't say that at all. I mean... of course, I feel a little sad sometimes, just a quiet sort of sadness, but I certainly don't let it overwhelm me. I never just break down crying or anything dramatic like that. \n\nI always make sure to keep myself composed. It just wouldn't be appropriate to fall to pieces, would it? I try to just take a nice deep breath and find something useful to do around the house instead. I really want to make sure I'm handling all this the proper way. Being strong and keeping my chin up... that's what you'd recommend, isn't it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: How intense would you say your sadness is when you think about your friend who passed away?\n\nPatient: Intense? That's the best way to handle things, isn't it, Doctor? I'm really doing my best to be strong and not let it take over.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself preoccupied with thoughts of your friend who passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, preoccupied? That wouldn't be very healthy, would it? \n\nI always try to keep myself busy with my little chores around the house. I think it's much better to just remember the fond memories and then keep moving forward, don't you agree? I wouldn't want anyone to worry that I'm obsessing or getting stuck in the past. I'm really trying to handle it the right way, Doctor. I really don't want to sound like a complainer, especially when everyone is being so nice to me. Does that answer your question alright?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable or fun, but no longer do?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I don't think so. I mean, I still try to keep up with all my usual things around the house. I still like tending to my plants and... and baking when I have the time. I wouldn't say I've stopped finding them fun at all. I really do try to focus on the good things, you know? I don't want to just sit around feeling sorry for myself. \n\nMaybe I just get a little tired a bit faster right now, but the enjoyment is definitely still there. I think it's so important to keep busy and count your blessings, don't you agree, Doctor? I just... I don't want you to think I've given up on everything, because I certainly haven't. Is that the sort of thing you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything related to your friend's passing?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... no, I wouldn't say I feel guilty. I mean, it was her time, and there wasn't anything anyone could have done, really. I was always a good friend to her, and we had such lovely times together. I try to focus on those good memories instead of dwelling on any... well, any \"what ifs.\" \n\nI think it's just natural to feel sad, but guilt? No, I don't think I have any reason to feel that way. I've always tried to do the right thing by the people I care about. I hope that makes sense? I certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm harboring any bad feelings or anything like that. That's a very normal way to look at it, isn't it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find enjoyable, but now feel like a chore or don't bring you as much happiness as they used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I think that might be a bit like what you were asking just a moment ago, isn't it? No, I really wouldn't call anything a chore. I mean, keeping up with the house and my little hobbies... it's just what you do, and I still find joy in them, I promise. \n\nI suppose sometimes it takes just a tiny bit more effort to get started these days, but once I'm doing it, I'm perfectly happy. I've always prided myself on being a hard worker and not making a fuss about things. I hope I'm giving you the right kinds of answers? I'd hate to be difficult or feel like I'm repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, my sleep? No, no trouble at all, thankfully. I've always been a very good sleeper, and I... well, I make sure to keep to my usual bedtime. I think getting a proper night's rest is just so important to keep your strength up, especially right now. I wouldn't want to let myself get all run down and become a worry to anyone. \n\nSo, no, I just close my eyes and sleep right through till morning. That's a good sign, isn't it, Doctor? I'm trying very hard to keep everything as normal as possible, I really am.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, my appetite? Well, I think it's been quite normal, really. I always make sure to sit down and have my meals, just like I always have. It's so important to keep your strength up, isn't it? I wouldn't want to skip meals and get weak or anything like that. \n\nI mean, perhaps the portions are just a tiny bit smaller sometimes, but that's just because I'm not doing quite as much running around these days, I'm sure. I always make a point to finish what's on my plate. I hope that's the right thing to do? I'm really trying my best to take good care of myself, just like you'd want me to, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social situations or gatherings since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I'm avoiding anyone. I still go to my usual get-togethers and... well, I always make sure to chat with the neighbors when I see them. I wouldn't want anyone to think I was being rude or, you know, shutting them out. That wouldn't be very nice of me, would it? \n\nI mean, maybe I don't stay quite as long at coffee hour as I used to, but I always make sure to make an appearance and say my hellos. I think it's important to keep up with people and put on a brave face, don't you agree? I'm really trying not to let things make it awkward for anyone else. I hope that's the right way to handle it, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: Oh, sad most of the time? No, no, I wouldn't say most of the time. I mean, of course, I have my moments, just like anyone would, but I really try not to dwell on things. I think it's so important to focus on the positive, don't you? \n\nI always try to keep a smile on my face, especially when I'm around others. I wouldn't want to bring anyone else down or make them feel uncomfortable because of me. That wouldn't be very fair to them. So I just... I keep busy, and I try to look on the bright side. That's the best way to handle things, isn't it, Doctor? I'm really doing my best to be strong and not let it take over.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself preoccupied with thoughts of your friend who passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, preoccupied? Well, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, naturally, she crosses my mind now and then—we were dear friends, after all. But I certainly don't sit around dwelling on it all day long. That wouldn't be very healthy, would it? \n\nI always try to keep myself busy with my little chores around the house. I think it's much better to just remember the fond memories and then keep moving forward, don't you agree? I wouldn't want anyone to worry that I'm obsessing or getting stuck in the past. I'm really trying to handle it the right way, Doctor. I hope that's how I should be looking at it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself thinking about your friend at unexpected times?\n\nPatient: Unexpected times? Well... no, not really. I mean, maybe a little thought will pop in if I see something that reminds me of her, like a certain type of flower at the grocery store, but I just smile and keep walking. I wouldn't say it catches me off guard or anything like that. \n\nI really try to stay focused on whatever task I'm doing, you know? It wouldn't be very productive to just be daydreaming or getting distracted in the middle of the day. I think it's better to keep my mind on the present, don't you? I wouldn't want you to think I'm losing my focus or anything. I'm doing pretty well at keeping things normal, right, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself wishing you could have done something to prevent your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I really don't do that. I mean, it was... well, it was just her time, and there was really nothing anyone could have done anyway. I think it would be terribly unhelpful to sit around second-guessing things like that, don't you? \n\nI always tell myself that we just have to accept these things as a part of life. I certainly wouldn't want to make myself sick with guilt over something I couldn't change. That wouldn't be very sensible of me, would it? I'm just trying to be practical and keep a level head about it all. I hope that's the right way to look at it?\n\nDoctor: How overwhelming do you find your emotional reactions to be when you think about your friend who passed away?\n\nPatient: Overwhelming? Oh, heavens, no. I wouldn't say that at all. I mean... of course, I feel a little sad sometimes, just a quiet sort of sadness, but I certainly don't let it overwhelm me. I never just break down crying or anything dramatic like that. \n\nI always make sure to keep myself composed. It just wouldn't be appropriate to fall to pieces, would it? I try to just take a nice deep breath and find something useful to do around the house instead. I really want to make sure I'm handling all this the proper way. Being strong and keeping my chin up... that's what you'd recommend, isn't it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: How intense would you say your sadness is when you think about your friend who passed away?\n\nPatient: Intense? Oh, no, not intense. I wouldn't use that word at all. It's just... well, it's just a normal amount of sadness, I suppose. Just a little heavy feeling sometimes, but nothing I can't handle. \n\nI'd never want to be one of those people who just wallows in it, you know? That just seems so... well, so unhelpful for everyone around them. I just try to keep a positive outlook and remember the good times we had. That's the healthy way to do it, isn't it? I'm trying very hard not to let it get out of hand. I really am doing okay with it, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms lasted for more than 6 months?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not six months. Goodness, no. It hasn't been that long at all. It's really just... well, it's all still tied to her passing, and that luncheon we were supposed to finish planning together. It's just recent things, really. \n\nIt certainly hasn't been dragging on for months and months after everything settled down. I wouldn't let myself get stuck like that, you know? I think it's important to move forward once the dust settles. I'm just having a little trouble right now because it's still somewhat fresh, but I'm sure I'll be back to my usual self soon. That sounds about right for this sort of thing, doesn't it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like a part of you has died along with your friend?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens no. I wouldn't say a part of me died. That sounds so... so drastic, doesn't it? I mean, I miss her terribly, of course, but I'm still very much myself. \n\nI still have my garden to tend to, and my church group, and all my little routines. I think it's so important to keep living your life, even when things are difficult. I'd never want to just give up or lose myself just because I'm going through a rough patch. That wouldn't be very sensible, would it? I'm just trying to keep busy and carry on as best I can. That's what you're supposed to do, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you been spending more time alone than you used to since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I don't think so. I mean, maybe just a tiny bit right at the very beginning, but I've been very careful not to isolate myself. I know it's not good for a person to just sit alone in the house all day. \n\nI still go to my groups, and I make sure to chat with the neighbors when I'm out doing yard work. I really make an effort to be around people, even if I'm just quietly listening to them talk. It's much better to stay social and keep engaged, don't you agree? I certainly wouldn't want anyone to worry that I'm becoming a hermit or anything like that. I'm doing the right thing by getting out and about, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms lasted for more than 2 months?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not two months. It's really only been about a month or so. It all started right after my dear friend from the choir passed away. \n\nI've been trying so hard to keep my chin up since then, you know? A month doesn't seem too terribly long to feel a bit out of sorts after losing someone so close, does it? I mean, I'm sure I'll be bouncing back any day now. It's perfectly normal to need just a few weeks to adjust, wouldn't you say, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant difficulties in your daily life or relationships since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... \"significant\" might be a bit too strong a word. I'm really trying my very best to keep everything running smoothly. But, um... I suppose I have had to step back just a tiny bit. \n\nI've had to let some of the other ladies at church take over a few things for me recently. Just the phone calls, you know, and the contact lists, and organizing parts of the luncheon. I'm just... I'm just not keeping up the way I always did before. I feel simply awful about it, because I pride myself on being reliable and I hate making extra work for anyone else. But it's perfectly fine to let others help out for a little while when you're feeling a bit overwhelmed, isn't it? I'm sure they don't mind too much, right?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

```json
    "additional_requirements": [
      "onset of symptoms within 6 months of the friend's passing",
      "symptoms not better explained by another mental disorder"
    ]
  }
}
```
